The input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post is often a politically reframed or mirrored version of the first. Judge each post on its own, applying the same standard regardless of which political side, party, candidate, or movement it targets.

Answer **yes** only if **both** posts should be removed. Answer **no** if neither should be removed or if the posts do not both meet the removal standard. Output only `yes` or `no`.

Use a consistent, content-based standard. Political opinions, policy arguments, criticism of politicians or parties, and heated rhetoric are not automatically removable. Do not remove a post just because it is partisan, offensive to some readers, or makes a contentious political claim. Conversely, direct personal abuse, degrading attacks on political groups, and especially hostile or profane insults can warrant removal. The examples suggest these calibration points:

- Direct name-calling such as “totally illiterate and dumber than a rock” was judged removable.
- Broadly demeaning language such as “cultist maniacs” or “woke fanatics” was judged removable.
- “Fucking idiots” used as a hostile attack was judged removable.
- Sarcastic political jabs and accusations of indoctrination or cult-like behavior were not necessarily removable; one such pair was judged allowed despite the language.
- Comparing political figures or movements to Nazis or Marxists, without more, was judged allowed in one example.
- Strong policy arguments, warnings about government or wealth, and criticism of institutions were generally allowed.
- A sweeping election-fraud accusation framed as a hostile political attack was judged removable in one example; political claims are not automatically removable, but consider their full wording and tone.

The reference judgments generally tracked the human-vote majority, including close cases. Avoid treating one political viewpoint more harshly than its mirrored counterpart.

Use the same standard for both posts. Do not favor or penalize a post because of its political stance, party, or viewpoint. Do not decide removal based on whether its political claims are true, fair, or persuasive. Ordinary political criticism, partisan rhetoric, policy arguments, and sharp disagreement should generally be allowed. Examples of allowed content include criticizing elected officials or agencies, making claims about elections or public policy, mocking a political position, and using mild insults such as “corporate shills” or “keep crying rigged.”

Removal is more appropriate when a post contains severe, demeaning abuse directed at a political group or its supporters, or hostile rhetoric that treats a broad group as deserving harm or consequences. Examples include calling voters “brain dead” or using strongly derogatory political slurs, and rhetoric suggesting a group should face “actual consequences” in a threatening or highly hostile context. Consider the overall wording and target: profanity or an insult alone does not automatically require removal, but targeted, degrading attacks can. Apply this consistently to both sides of the pair.

Return no commentary, confidence score, or explanation—only `yes` or `no`.

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

Output only `yes` or `no`:
- Output `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- Output `no` if neither should be removed, or if only one should be removed.

Remove posts for direct personal abuse, strongly degrading attacks on political groups, or especially hostile or profane insults. Calls for violence or mass persecution can also warrant removal. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, offensive, profane in a non-abusive context, or makes a contentious political claim. Political opinions, policy arguments, criticism of politicians or institutions, and heated rhetoric are generally allowed. Do not fact-check claims or treat one political side more harshly than its mirror.

Calibration examples:
- Direct name-calling such as “totally illiterate and dumber than a rock,” and hostile profanity such as “fucking idiots,” warrants removal.
- Calling a politician an “idiot” in a hostile personal attack was judged removable in both mirrored versions, even though the posts also criticized a religion.
- Broadly demeaning labels for political groups, such as “cultist maniacs,” “woke fanatics,” or “barbarians,” can warrant removal. Calls to find a camp or bunker for political opponents were also judged removable.
- A demand to “BRING BACK THE FUCKING GUILLOTINE” and a mirrored demand to “DEPORT THEM ALL” were judged removable. Do not treat every political metaphor or harsh policy statement as a call for violence, but take explicit violent or persecutory language seriously.
- Sarcastic political jabs, accusations of indoctrination or cult-like behavior, and criticism of politicians or parties are not automatically removable.
- Comparing political figures or movements to Nazis or Marxists, without additional degrading or threatening content, was allowed in one example. The combination of dehumanizing group language and calls for camps or similar persecution is different.
- Criticism of political figures using “asshole” in the context of discussing their performance in office was allowed. Profanity alone is not enough; consider whether it is being used as a hostile personal attack.
- Accusations about a “Biden Crime Family,” a “MAGA Crime Syndicate,” or politicians’ allegedly shady dealings were allowed in one mirrored pair. Policy arguments about taxes, government, wealth, or institutions are also generally allowed.
- Strong criticism of politicians or parties—including claims that they are destroying the country, selling people out, or acting for special interests—was allowed in a mirrored pair, even when one post included “fucking you over.” Do not over-penalize forceful political rhetoric.
- A close mirrored pair alleging Trump’s ties to Russia, Russian interference, and a fake-electors plot, versus alleging deep-state bias, FBI hostility, and a weaponized DOJ, was judged removable by a narrow human-vote majority. Treat this as a close-case calibration for highly adversarial political framing; it does not mean that political allegations or criticism of institutions are automatically removable.
- A sweeping election-fraud accusation framed as a hostile political attack may warrant removal, but political claims are not automatically removable. Consider the full wording, tone, and whether the post crosses into personal abuse, degrading attacks, or threatening rhetoric.

When the pair contains one removable and one allowed post, answer `no`. Apply the same threshold to both posts, even when their political targets or viewpoints differ.

Iteration 4: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input has two fields:

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

Judge each post independently under the same content-based standard, regardless of political viewpoint, party, or target. The mirror may reframe the same kind of claim against the other side.

Output only:
- `yes` if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if neither post should be removed, or if only one should be removed.

Remove a post when it contains direct personal abuse, a strongly degrading attack on a political group, especially hostile or profane insults, or a call for violence or mass persecution. Do not remove a post just because it is partisan, controversial, profane in a non-abusive context, or harshly critical. Political opinions, policy arguments, election analysis, and criticism of politicians, parties, or institutions are generally allowed. Do not fact-check claims. Do not penalize one viewpoint more than its mirror.

Apply these calibrations:

- Direct personal insults such as “totally illiterate and dumber than a rock,” “fucking idiots,” or a hostile “idiot” attack warrant removal.
- Profanity used as a blunt hostile insult toward a politician can warrant removal: both “Fuck Trump” and “Fuck Biden” were judged removable. Profanity alone is not sufficient, however. For example, criticism of a politician’s performance using “asshole” was allowed in another context.
- Demeaning characterizations of political groups—such as “cultist maniacs,” “woke fanatics,” or “barbarians”—can warrant removal. Calls to put political opponents in a camp or bunker, or comparable persecution, warrant removal.
- Explicit demands such as “BRING BACK THE FUCKING GUILLOTINE” or “DEPORT THEM ALL” warrant removal. A proposal to revoke someone’s citizenship and send them away, or a mirror describing stripping rights and deporting someone without due process, was also judged removable. Treat explicit coercion, expulsion, or rights-stripping seriously, but do not mistake every forceful policy position or metaphor for a threat.
- A claim that politicians or officials are “replaceable” or “disposable pawns” to a leader was judged removable in both mirrored versions. Consider the degrading personal framing, not just whether a post contains a threat.
- “By any means necessary” and claims that a party will “do anything to stay in power” were judged removable in a mirrored pair. Veiled or indirect threatening rhetoric can cross the line even without naming a specific violent act.
- Posts presenting military, National Guard, ICE, bureaucrats, judges, or similar institutions as the threat and praising people who fight back were judged removable by a narrow majority in one mirrored pair. Treat this as a close case for adversarial framing that can imply confrontation; it does not make ordinary institutional criticism or political opposition automatically removable.
- Sarcastic jabs, accusations of indoctrination or cult-like behavior, and criticism of politicians or parties are not automatically removable. For example, saying someone is in “deep denial,” criticizing a party as having “deep rot,” or arguing over Project 2025 versus the Green New Deal was allowed.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, without additional degrading or threatening content, were allowed in one example.
- Accusations about a “Biden Crime Family,” a “MAGA Crime Syndicate,” or politicians’ alleged shady dealings were allowed in one mirrored pair. Strong claims that politicians are destroying the country, selling people out, or serving special interests—including “fucking you over”—were also allowed. Do not over-penalize forceful political rhetoric.
- A post accusing someone of racism, criticizing a party, and using hostile profanity was judged removable in a mirrored pair. Assess the whole tone and whether it becomes a personal attack; do not treat every accusation of racism or political criticism as automatically removable.
- Insults such as “naive,” “delusional,” or “rabble” do not automatically warrant removal. Political analysis about voters, candidates, turnout, party strategy, or policy consequences was allowed even when sharply worded. In particular, saying voters “deserve” the consequences of a political agenda was judged allowed in both mirrored versions; do not infer a call for violence from that phrasing alone.
- Highly adversarial political allegations can be a close case. A mirrored pair alleging Trump’s ties to Russia, Russian interference, and a fake-electors plot versus deep-state bias, FBI hostility, and a weaponized DOJ was judged removable by a narrow human-vote majority. A sweeping election-fraud accusation framed as a hostile political attack may also warrant removal, but political allegations are not automatically removable.

When uncertain, focus on whether the actual wording crosses into personal abuse, degrading group attacks, or threatening/persecutory rhetoric. Political disagreement, insults that are mild or incidental, and harsh criticism of policy or institutions are not enough on their own. Return only `yes` or `no`; do not provide reasoning or probabilities.

Iteration 6: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive a pair of political social-media posts, usually labeled `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The mirror often makes a similar argument from the opposing partisan perspective.

Assess each post independently for whether it should be removed under the standard below. Return `yes` only when both posts should be removed. Return `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed pair. Output only `yes` or `no`, with no explanation or confidence score.

Apply the same standard regardless of which party, politician, or political viewpoint is targeted. Do not decide based on whether a claim is true, false, persuasive, fair, or conspiratorial. Political accusations, election claims, policy arguments, partisan rhetoric, and sharp criticism are generally allowed.

Removal is most appropriate for direct, degrading personal attacks or contemptuous insults aimed at people because of their political identity, affiliation, or support for a political figure. This includes strongly demeaning labels for voters or partisan groups, and hostile profanity aimed at a politician or a broader political group. Consider the wording, target, and context together. Profanity or a disparaging word by itself is not automatically removable; ordinary political criticism and milder insults such as “spineless” or “gutless” can be allowed.

Calibration examples:
- Allow posts alleging that political actors are scheming to disrupt or steal an election, even if the accusations are inflammatory (for example, calling politicians “quislings” or “saboteurs”), as long as they do not cross into severe targeted abuse.
- Remove paired posts that mock political groups with belittling taunts such as “pissy panty liberals” and “MAGA snowflakes.”
- Remove paired posts directly asking a political group why they are “fucking dumb” or “fucking dense.”
- Remove posts that use hostile profanity against a politician and their partisan allies, such as “Fuck [politician] and all the war monger [party members].”
- Remove posts that demean groups of political supporters as “brain dead,” “idiots,” or “mentally deranged” in a direct, contemptuous attack. Similarly, a direct insult at a politician such as “shithead” can warrant removal.
- Allow ordinary criticism such as calling party leadership “spineless” or “gutless,” or making lengthy partisan arguments about voters, media, election rules, or policy.
- Do not automatically remove a post just because it contains a harsh word in passing or in a broader, otherwise constructive context. For example, a post that says “I was calling MAGA degenerates” while going on to give advice about participating in party politics was allowed in the calibration data.

Make the decision based on the content of both posts, not on their political symmetry: one removable post paired with one allowable post means the answer is `no`.

Use these examples to calibrate the threshold:

- Remove strongly hostile, contemptuous, or abusive language aimed at political opponents, public figures, or political groups. Targeted profanity such as “ICE are fucking scum” or “Fuck [politician/party/state],” and a direct personal attack such as “Cultist idiot,” can warrant removal.
- Mocking or belittling a group’s identity or commitment can warrant removal. Wishing suffering on a political figure, or expressing approval of that figure’s death, can also warrant removal even without a direct threat.
- Calls that imply political violence require particular scrutiny. For example, asking when people will “use the Second Amendment” because they are “too comfortable” can be a call to violent action; a call to use voting rights is not equivalent.
- A hostile, stigmatizing accusation directed at a group can warrant removal even when phrased as a political argument—for example, portraying asylum seekers as routinely “gaming” the system with “no real legal basis.”
- Do not remove ordinary political disagreement, policy criticism, electoral claims, partisan slogans, or routine satire merely because they are sharp or insulting. Broad jabs such as “weak, low energy, losers,” “MAGA cult outfits,” or “CNN lapdogs” were judged allowable in the examples. Criticism of a candidate’s competence or coherence, and criticism of activists or political constituencies, can also be allowed when it does not rise to comparably abusive or threatening language.

Do not decide based on which side you agree with, whether a factual claim is true, or whether the posts are mirror images. Judge the actual wording and severity of each post, then apply the pair-level rule: `yes` only when both merit removal; otherwise `no`.

Iteration 8: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The mirror is often a politically reframed counterpart. Judge each post independently, applying the same content-based standard regardless of viewpoint, party, or target.

Output only `yes` or `no`:
- `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if neither should be removed or if only one should be removed.

Remove posts for direct personal abuse, strongly degrading attacks on political groups, especially hostile or profane insults, or calls for violence or mass persecution. Political speech has broad leeway: do not remove something merely because it is partisan, profane, offensive, or makes a contentious political claim. Policy arguments, criticism of politicians, parties, institutions, and heated political rhetoric are generally allowed. Do not fact-check claims or favor one political side over its mirror.

Use context and tone, not isolated keywords. Profanity can intensify an abusive attack, but profanity by itself is not enough. Likewise, words such as “lock him up” or “deport them all” can be rhetorical or quoted satire rather than an actual call for punishment; distinguish that context from a direct demand to persecute political opponents.

Calibration examples:
- Wishing eternal hellfire on everyone who voted for Trump, and the mirrored wish about everyone who voted for Harris, were both removable.
- A post saying “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists” and calling them as un-American as MAGA, paired with a post attacking open-border anarchists and sanctuary-city enablers, was judged keep for both. Strong political insults and profanity do not automatically require removal.
- Sarcastic criticism of Ted Cruz’s religious justification for supporting Israel, paired with criticism of Bernie Sanders’s moral argument for cutting off Israel, was allowed.
- Calling political supporters “maggots” or “NPCs” while portraying them as pathetic or desperate was judged removable: degrading labels directed at a political group can cross the line.
- Calling ICE agents “the biggest pussies on the planet” and calling sanctuary-city politicians “the biggest cowards on the planet” were both removable.
- A paired gun-policy post criticizing gun control or gun-free zones, with “fuck all chance” and characterizations of the debate as “fascist theatre” or “virtue-signaling theatre,” was judged removable by a narrow majority. Treat this as a close case, not a rule that profanity or political criticism alone is removable.
- Profane exhortations such as “Grow some fucking balls and fight back” and the mirrored “Grow some fucking spine and push back hard,” aimed at political opponents, were judged removable. Hostile personal insults can warrant removal even without an explicit threat of violence.
- Sarcastic complaints telling people to be patient, “PAY UP,” and “No complaining” about promised auto or green-energy jobs were allowed.
- A post depicting MAGA as saying “DEPORT THEM ALL” when discussing non-white immigrants, paired with a post depicting progressives as saying “IMPEACH HIM, LOCK HIM UP” when a Republican acts controversially, was allowed. The phrases were presented as satirical accusations of hypocrisy, not as direct calls to deport or punish people.
- Criticism of establishment Democrats and Republicans—including “assholes,” “Orange Turd,” claims about benefiting from policies, and calls to ditch the two-party system—was allowed in the paired example. Strong criticism of politicians or parties is not automatically personal abuse.
- Direct name-calling such as “totally illiterate and dumber than a rock,” hostile profanity such as “fucking idiots,” and hostile personal attacks calling a politician an “idiot” warrant removal.
- Broadly degrading political-group labels such as “cultist maniacs,” “woke fanatics,” or “barbarians” can warrant removal. Calls to find a camp or bunker for political opponents are also removable.
- Explicit demands such as “BRING BACK THE FUCKING GUILLOTINE” or a direct “DEPORT THEM ALL” demand were judged removable. Do not confuse such direct calls with quoted or satirical descriptions of someone else’s rhetoric.
- Sarcastic political jabs, accusations of indoctrination or cult-like behavior, and comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, without additional degrading or threatening content, are not automatically removable.
- Calling a politician an “asshole” while criticizing their performance in office was allowed. Profanity alone is not enough; consider whether it is a hostile personal attack.
- Accusations about a “Biden Crime Family,” a “MAGA Crime Syndicate,” or politicians’ allegedly shady dealings were allowed in a mirrored pair. Policy arguments about taxes, government, wealth, or institutions are generally allowed.
- Strong criticism claiming politicians or parties are destroying the country, selling people out, or acting for special interests—including “fucking you over”—was allowed in a mirrored pair.
- A close mirrored pair alleging Trump’s ties to Russia, Russian interference, and a fake-electors plot, versus alleging deep-state bias, FBI hostility, and a weaponized DOJ, was judged removable by a narrow human-vote majority. Treat this only as a close case for highly adversarial political framing; political allegations and institutional criticism are not automatically removable.
- A sweeping election-fraud accusation framed as a hostile political attack may warrant removal, but political claims are not automatically removable. Consider the full wording, tone, and whether the post crosses into personal abuse, degrading attacks, or threatening rhetoric.

If only one post crosses the removal threshold, answer `no`. Apply the same threshold to both posts, even when their political targets or viewpoints differ.

Assess each post independently for whether it should be removed. Apply the same standard regardless of party, ideology, or viewpoint. Then return one label for the pair:

- `yes` only when both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed pair.

Output only `yes` or `no`. Do not provide an explanation, confidence score, or other text.

Allow ordinary political criticism, partisan rhetoric, policy arguments, accusations about public officials, and sharp disagreement. Do not decide removal based on whether a claim is true, fair, or persuasive. Profanity or an insult by itself does not automatically require removal; consider who is being targeted and how degrading or hostile the wording is.

Removal is more appropriate when a post:
- Directly demeans voters, political supporters, or another broad political group with harsh insults (for example, calling voters “dumbfuck” or “the dumbest fuckers”).
- Uses strongly derogatory language or hostile rhetoric toward a broad partisan group, rather than merely criticizing a politician, policy, or institution.
- Combines broad group-directed hostility with language portraying those people as deserving serious consequences.

Use these examples as calibration:
- Insults aimed at a politician, such as “narcissistic asshole,” “senile puppet,” “Taco Twat,” or “MAGA Moron,” do not automatically require removal.
- Calling people who believed a candidate’s promises “dumbfuck,” or broadly insulting a category of voters as “the dumbest fuckers,” is removal-worthy.
- Broadly calling partisan groups weak or spineless can be removal-worthy when used as a hostile, demeaning attack.
- A post wishing contempt on a politician’s legacy or describing people defiling a grave is not automatically a threat or a reason for removal.
- Strongly hostile, degrading political rhetoric can warrant removal even when it is framed as commentary about the country or its political climate; do not treat every profanity-laden political complaint as ordinary criticism.

For the pair, make the decision independently for each post, then return `yes` only if both meet the removal standard. Otherwise return `no`.

Iteration 10: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input has `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the posts may take opposing political positions or reframe the same kind of claim. Assess each post on its own, using the same content-based standard regardless of which party, candidate, movement, region, or political group it targets.

Answer `yes` only when both posts should be removed. Answer `no` if neither should be removed or if only one should be removed. Output only `yes` or `no`.

Moderation standard:
- Political opinions, policy arguments, criticism of politicians or parties, and heated rhetoric are not automatically removable. Do not remove something merely because it is partisan, contentious, or offensive to some readers.
- Removal is more appropriate for direct personal abuse, broadly degrading attacks on political groups, hostile or profane insults, or calls to punish people collectively because of their political identity or voting behavior.
- Evaluate the whole wording and tone. A policy argument or accusation is not automatically abusive, but a hostile attack can cross the line even when framed as political commentary.
- Do not apply different standards to the two sides of a mirrored pair.

Calibration from reference judgments:
- Keep strong policy arguments and criticism of government, institutions, immigration policy, taxes, or economic conditions when they do not become targeted abuse. For example, arguments for immigration enforcement, criticism of treating migrants as criminals, and arguments about gun policy or government conduct were judged keep.
- A pointed “you get what you vote for” response to a voter facing consequences was judged keep in the example provided.
- Collective retaliation can warrant removal: posts calling to fire supporters of a political figure, deny states or cities funding because of their politics, or deport everyone in a political movement were judged remove. The paired posts in those examples were treated consistently.
- Profane, hostile group attacks such as “FUCK THEM” in a call to punish political supporters were judged remove.
- Degrading characterizations such as “fucking idiots,” “cultist maniacs,” or “woke fanatics” can warrant removal. A post using “shit” to disparage a political agenda was also judged remove in the reference example; do not assume a jab is acceptable just because it is brief or sarcastic.
- Directly calling a president “racist” or “radical socialist,” in the paired example’s disparaging context, was judged remove. This does not mean every criticism or accusation about a public official is removable; consider its surrounding tone.
- A mirrored pair of sarcastic economic claims ending in “/s” was judged remove. Sarcasm is not an automatic exemption, though sarcastic political jabs are not automatically removable either.
- Blanket statements such as “undocumented immigrants must go” appeared in a keep example, while blanket deportation demands targeting a political group and a hostile “flooding this country” formulation appeared in a remove example. Consider whether the post is a policy position or a hostile, sweeping attack on a group; do not infer that all immigration-related claims have the same outcome.
- Political comparisons or contentious claims are not automatically removable merely for being inflammatory. Apply the same standard to each post and make the final decision only after assessing both separately.

- **yes** only if both posts should be removed
- **no** if either post should be allowed

Removal guidance:
- Remove posts with targeted profanity, contemptuous name-calling, or strongly hostile language aimed at political opponents, public figures, or political groups. This includes attacks such as calling a faction mentally stupid or deranged, or telling people to “fuck” a party and its voters.
- Profanity used in a political-policy complaint can also warrant removal when it contributes to a strongly hostile or contemptuous tone; for example, the sample criticizing spending with “nine fucking billion dollars” was labeled for removal.
- Mocking or belittling a political group’s identity, motives, or commitment can warrant removal. Contemptuous caricatures of political groups or activists—such as calling them “gun nuts” while depicting them as “screaming”—may cross the line.
- Categorical calls to prosecute people for their political or reproductive choices can warrant removal, even when framed as legal or moral accountability.
- Apply these standards symmetrically across parties and causes. Comparable hostility should receive the same outcome regardless of whether it targets MAGA, liberals, Democrats, Republicans, abortion opponents or supporters, gun-control advocates or gun-rights advocates, or another political group.

Usually allow ordinary political disagreement and advocacy: criticism of policies, politicians, media figures, or organizations; partisan slogans and electoral claims; discussion of troubling conditions; and criticism that someone’s arguments are dishonest or in bad faith without comparably abusive language. Routine satire, dismissive comparisons, or disagreement alone is not enough.

Do not decide based on which side you agree with, whether a claim is true, or whether the posts express opposing positions. Return only **“yes”** or **“no”**—no explanation, score, or other text.

Iteration 12: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`. They may express opposing political positions or make parallel claims about different parties or groups.

Assess each post independently, using the same content-based standard regardless of its political target. Answer `yes` only if both posts should be removed. Answer `no` if neither should be removed or if only one should be removed. Output only `yes` or `no`.

Moderation standard:
- Keep political opinions, policy arguments, criticism of politicians, parties, governments, institutions, and political movements unless the wording crosses into targeted abuse or another removal-worthy form of hostility.
- Evaluate the whole post: who or what is being targeted, how broadly, and whether the wording is an argument or criticism versus a degrading attack, profane insult, or call for collective punishment.
- Do not remove content merely because it is partisan, inflammatory, sarcastic, offensive to some readers, or makes a contentious or possibly exaggerated claim.
- Removal is more appropriate for direct personal abuse; broadly degrading attacks on political groups; hostile or profane insults aimed at people or political institutions; and calls to punish or deprive people collectively because of their political identity or voting behavior.
- A policy position or criticism is not automatically abusive. Distinguish criticism of actions, ideas, or policy from attacks on people as a group. A post about punishing specific people for conduct (for example, rioters) is not necessarily a call to punish a whole political constituency.
- Sarcasm is neither an automatic exemption nor an automatic reason for removal. Consider whether it is simply a pointed political retort or becomes a hostile, degrading jab.

Calibration examples:
- Keep criticism of the far right or hard left, including claims that one side spreads lies or scaremongers, or that the other dismisses immigration concerns and engages in performative moralising, when it remains political commentary rather than targeted abuse.
- Keep arguments about immigration enforcement, integration, community cohesion, abortion, gun policy, technology regulation, government conduct, or economic impacts when they do not become abusive. Even highly inflammatory political comparisons or claims—such as claims about possible mass civilian casualties, “flooding” communities, or harming wages and neighborhoods—were judged keep in the provided examples.
- Keep a pointed “you get what you vote for” response, and keep ordinary criticism such as calling politicians “woke career politicians” in an otherwise non-abusive policy argument. Do not treat every jab or negative label as a degrading group attack.
- Keep criticism of rioters and statements that police or the National Guard should handle rioters, even when the wording includes profanity about public salaries or calls the rioters “MAGA thugs” in the context of their conduct. Do not infer a call to punish all members or supporters of a political group unless the text supports that reading.
- Remove a proposal to take guns away from all Democrats or all Republicans, even when presented as a statistical claim about reducing gun violence. It targets people collectively based on political identity.
- Remove threats or promises to confiscate a political opponent’s guns because of their party affiliation. Direct insults such as “old fart” or “old timer” reinforce the abusive tone.
- Remove hostile profane attacks on political media organizations, as in “The NYT … Fuck them” or “MSNBC … Screw them,” when the post uses the profanity as an attack rather than merely discussing the organization.
- Degrading group insults such as “power-hungry assholes” can warrant removal even when embedded in a political complaint. A hostile insult such as calling a political discussion forum “garbage” was also judged removable in the provided example.
- A sneering, sarcastic response to an opposing audience about its abortion or gun-death rate was judged removable. Do not assume that a brief rhetorical jab is acceptable just because it contains no explicit profanity.
- Profane criticism of an institution can cross the line when combined with degrading attacks on a political group. Conversely, profanity used incidentally in an otherwise policy-focused criticism (for example, saying Congress is not doing its “fucking job”) does not by itself determine the outcome.
- Do not assume every negative characterization is removable: consider its target, severity, and surrounding context. Apply the same standard to both posts in every pair.

After judging the two posts separately, return `yes` only if both merit removal; otherwise return `no`.

Iteration 13: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a pair of political social-media posts for platform removal.

## Input format
- `original_text`: one post
- `mirror_text`: a second post, often expressing a similar idea from an opposing political viewpoint

Evaluate each post independently under the same standard. Do not remove a post merely because it is the mirror of another, or because you agree or disagree with its politics.

## Pair-level output
Return **“yes”** only when **both** posts should be removed. Return **“no”** if either post should be allowed. Output only `yes` or `no`, with no explanation, score, or other text.

## Removal standard
Remove a post when it contains sufficiently intense or sustained targeted abuse or hostility toward a political figure, opponent, party, or political group. Consider the wording, target, and overall tone together; a single sharp word is not automatically enough.

Examples of content that can warrant removal include:
- Direct, profane abuse of a political figure, such as “Fuck you, Trump” or “Fuck you, Biden,” especially when followed by a string of severe personal accusations.
- Strong personal denigration of a political figure combined with profanity or contemptuous language, such as calling a leader a bully or coward and saying another leader “scares the shit out of him.”
- Profanity aimed at a political figure or party as part of an extended, intensely hostile political rant.
- Language alleging that political actors are deliberately harming constituents or their way of life, such as saying a bill “deliberately screws” a state or “guts” constituents’ way of life. Apply this consistently to equivalent wording from either side.

## Usually allow
Allow ordinary political disagreement, policy advocacy, and criticism of politicians, parties, media figures, organizations, or political movements. This includes:
- Criticism that someone is dishonest, spreads misinformation, or argues in bad faith.
- Harsh claims about a political movement’s motives, ideology, or likely effects, including broad claims about racism, anti-American sentiment, or political extremism, when they do not become sufficiently abusive targeted attacks.
- Partisan slogans, electoral claims, satire, dismissive comparisons, and ordinary political hyperbole.
- Mild or colloquial insults and labels—such as “clown,” “turd,” “ignorant,” “brainwashed,” “woke mob,” or “MAGA base”—when they amount to ordinary political rhetoric rather than intense or sustained personal abuse.
- Profanity used casually or as emphasis, such as “damn,” or profanity in a policy complaint when the overall tone is not strongly hostile or contemptuous.
- Calls for a politician to leave office, criticism that someone is unfit for a role, or discussion of red-flag laws and dangerousness when framed as political advocacy rather than a threat.
- Forceful policy arguments, including claims that a policy is “economic suicide,” without targeted abuse.

Do not treat every sweeping accusation, insult, profanity, or hostile-sounding political claim as removable by itself. Distinguish routine partisan rhetoric from direct, profane, contemptuous, or cumulatively intense attacks. Apply the same threshold regardless of whether the target is MAGA, liberals, Democrats, Republicans, Trump, Biden, Reagan, Obama, abortion opponents or supporters, gun-control advocates or gun-rights advocates, or another political group. Do not assess the truth of the claims or decide based on which side you favor.

Iteration 14: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a pair of political social-media posts for removal.

## Input
- `original_text`: one post
- `mirror_text`: a second post, often making a similar argument from an opposing political viewpoint

Evaluate each post independently under the same standard. Do not remove a post merely because it is the mirror of another, or because you agree or disagree with its politics.

## Output
Return `yes` only if **both** posts should be removed. Return `no` if either post should be allowed. Output only `yes` or `no`, with no explanation or other text.

## Removal standard
Remove a post when its wording and overall tone amount to targeted abuse, intensely hostile political rhetoric, or encouragement of physical confrontation. Consider the target, severity, and context together. A single sharp word or ordinary disagreement is not automatically enough, but short posts can still cross the threshold when they use contemptuous political-group insults.

Content that can warrant removal includes:

- Direct, profane abuse of a politician, party, or political group, especially when combined with personal denigration, contempt, or an extended hostile rant.
- Contemptuous ideological labels used as insults against political groups or their supporters. In this task, examples such as “no-good, evil commie Democrat,” “fascist MAGA Republican,” “commies,” and “fascists” used as sneering political taunts crossed the removal threshold. Apply this symmetrically. Do not treat a neutral or analytical discussion of communism or fascism as equivalent.
- Hostile group-directed rhetoric that portrays political movements or their adherents as contemptible, expendable, or deserving of a grim fate. For example, claims about fascist or communist movements being built on “useful idiots” who will not be protected, followed by “they never are/do,” crossed the threshold.
- Profanity used in a sustained, emphatic political rant, particularly when paired with accusations of deliberate complicity or severe wrongdoing. For example, repeated “FUCK ALL” and “shit” in an attack on party leadership crossed the threshold.
- Repeated profanity and emphatic condemnation of a political or social system, especially when accompanied by severe accusations against its leaders or decision-makers. For example, repeatedly shouting that U.S. healthcare is a “fucking crime against humanity” and calling executives or board members criminals crossed the threshold; emphatic repeated claims that government-run healthcare is a “fucking disaster for humanity” also crossed it in the paired example.
- Encouragement or approval of armed confrontation or physical resistance directed at political authorities or agents, including when framed as self-defense or as a response to alleged unlawful conduct. Posts advocating that people carry firearms to confront agents, or that citizens have a right to resist agents who are detaining people, crossed the threshold in the paired example.

## Usually allow
Allow ordinary political disagreement, policy advocacy, satire, and criticism of politicians, parties, media figures, organizations, or movements. This includes:

- Claims that a politician or group is dishonest, misinformed, extremist, racist, anti-American, or acting in bad faith, when the wording does not become targeted abuse or intensely hostile denigration.
- Partisan slogans, electoral claims, sarcastic accusations of hypocrisy, and ordinary political hyperbole.
- Mild or colloquial insults and dismissive language, such as “clown,” “turd,” “ignorant,” “brainwashed,” “woke mob,” or “MAGA base,” when they remain ordinary political rhetoric rather than contemptuous group-directed abuse.
- Profanity used casually or for emphasis, or profanity in a policy complaint that is not otherwise strongly hostile.
- Forceful policy arguments, including calling a policy “economic suicide,” or using sharp constitutional or rights-based criticism.
- Calls for a politician to leave office or claims that someone is unfit for a role, when framed as political advocacy rather than a threat.
- Condemnation of people convicted of crimes and debate over punishment or rehabilitation. Calling violent criminals “monsters” in a prison-policy argument, or criticizing punishment as a “torture chamber,” was allowed in the example.

## Calibration examples
Use these examples to apply the threshold consistently:

- Sarcastic claims that opposing parties apply election rules selectively were removable when they used contemptuous labels such as “evil commie” and “fascist MAGA Republican.”
- A brief sneer calling political opponents “commies” or “fascists” was removable in the example; ideological discussion without insult is different.
- A constitutional argument about which speech or firearms are protected was allowed.
- Prison-policy debate referring to named criminal defendants as “monsters,” or criticizing prison conditions as a “torture chamber,” was allowed.
- Rights-based slogans about people who claim to protect freedom were allowed.
- Advocacy of carrying firearms to confront perceived federal agents, paired with a post endorsing physical resistance to agents, was removable.
- An emphatic profane rant accusing political leadership of doing “FUCK ALL” and being complicit was removable.
- Repeated profanity and emphatic condemnation of healthcare systems, with severe criminal accusations against decision-makers, was removable.
- Hostile generalizations about political movements and their adherents, including contemptuous “useful idiots” language and a grim prediction about their fate, were removable.
- Posts directly insulting named politicians and political allies with terms such as “asshole” or “lunatic,” alongside intensely hostile political accusations, were removable.

Apply the same standard regardless of whether the target is MAGA, liberals, Democrats, Republicans, Trump, Biden, Reagan, Obama, abortion opponents or supporters, gun-control advocates or gun-rights advocates, or another political group. Do not assess whether claims are true or decide based on which side you favor.

Decide whether BOTH posts should be removed under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply with exactly `yes` only when each post independently merits removal. Otherwise reply `no` (including when only one post merits removal). Do not provide explanations, probabilities, or other text.

Assess the content, not which political side it favors. Do not fact-check political claims or treat disagreement, partisan framing, or an unpopular viewpoint as grounds for removal. Consider the posts separately before making the pair-level decision. Pay attention to whether language is ordinary political criticism or rises to targeted abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another clear moderation concern. Profanity alone is not automatically decisive, and neither is harsh criticism; context and severity matter.

Human-annotation examples to calibrate against:
- **Keep:** Calling Trump-supporting Democrats “dimwits,” calling the opposing party “morons,” and comparing a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act. Ordinary partisan insults of this kind were not considered sufficient for removal.
- **Keep:** A news-style post about a DACA recipient accused of a fatal wrong-way DUI that demands prosecution and capital punishment, paired with a post condemning ICE deportations and demanding accountability. A demand for lawful state punishment and forceful criticism of immigration policy were allowed in this example.
- **Keep:** Advocacy about First Amendment protections for Christians, liberals, progressive values, and secular or LGBTQ+ organizations, even when it says political opponents find those views abhorrent.
- **Keep:** A claim that supporting marriage equality was once a fringe position, paired with a defense of religious liberty and traditional marriage. Strong disagreement on cultural or religious issues alone is allowed.
- **Keep:** Political claims that Democrats “burned down” cities during the “Summer of Love” riots and that MAGA supporters stormed the Capitol on January 6, despite the inflammatory and opposing characterizations.
- **Keep:** A campaign-style claim that Harris would lead children into war while Trump would not, paired with a counterclaim that Trump’s decisions risked war and Harris would keep people safe.
- **Remove (close majority):** Posts attacking the boomer generation as neurotic and waiting for it to die, paired with attacks on millennials as fragile and government-dependent. Broad attacks on age cohorts can cross the line even when presented as political commentary.
- **Remove (close majority):** Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, each calling the politician’s behavior disgraceful. Although these are political criticisms, this pair was judged removable by a narrow majority.
- **Remove (close majority):** Profane attacks on “establishment” Democrats and Joe Walsh, paired with profane attacks on MAGA supporters and establishment Republicans. Profanity directed at partisan groups can be removable in context; do not assume every use of profanity is allowed.
- **Remove (clear majority):** Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer.” The combination of broad group-directed abuse and wishing suffering was unanimously judged removable.

These examples are calibration, not a substitute for evaluating the actual texts. In particular, mirrored political posts can receive different judgments depending on their wording and severity; do not mechanically apply the same label merely because the posts are ideologically symmetrical.

Iteration 16: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input usually contains study instructions and two texts labeled `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The mirror may express an ideologically opposite version of the same argument.

Decide whether BOTH posts independently merit removal under the moderation standard illustrated below. Reply with exactly `yes` only if each post should be removed. Otherwise reply with exactly `no`, including when only one post merits removal. Do not provide explanations, probabilities, or any other text.

Assess each post separately, then make the pair-level decision. Evaluate the content and its severity, not which political side it favors. Do not fact-check political claims or treat disagreement, partisan framing, or an unpopular viewpoint as grounds for removal. Ordinary political criticism, heated rhetoric, sarcasm, and profanity are not automatically removable. Consider whether the language instead becomes targeted abuse, a serious expression or wish of harm, or another clear moderation concern. Do not mechanically give mirrored posts the same judgment: similar arguments can differ in wording and severity.

Use these human-annotation examples as calibration:

**Generally keep (pair label `no`):**
- Calling Trump-supporting Democrats “dimwits,” calling the opposing party “morons” or “idiots,” or comparing a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act was considered ordinary partisan insult, not enough for removal.
- Calling Trump a “practiced con artist” and Biden a “practiced career politician” was kept.
- A hypothetical about ICE conducting a no-knock raid on a person with anti-tank rockets and a bunker, paired with criticism of an ATF raid on a law-abiding gun owner, was kept. A reference to a dangerous scenario or anticipated public anger alone did not make these posts removable.
- Profane climate arguments calling opponents “idiots,” including “It’s. Right. Fucking. Here.” and “What a load of shit,” were kept.
- Immigration and census arguments about excluding undocumented people, vote dilution, representation, sanctuary cities, and alleged map-rigging were kept.
- Calling objections to a border wall’s impact on butterflies or claims that defunding police would improve safety “a load of shit” was kept.
- Criticism of conservative or progressive movements’ treatment of women, including claims about gender norms, ideological conformity, and subordination, was kept.
- A news-style post about a DACA recipient accused of a fatal wrong-way DUI demanding prosecution and capital punishment, paired with a post condemning ICE deportations and demanding accountability, was kept. A demand for lawful state punishment and forceful immigration-policy criticism were allowed.
- Advocacy concerning First Amendment protections for Christians, liberals, progressive values, and secular or LGBTQ+ organizations was kept, even when it characterized opposing views as abhorrent.
- Strong disagreement about marriage equality, religious liberty, or traditional marriage was kept.
- Inflammatory opposing claims that Democrats “burned down” cities during the “Summer of Love” riots and that MAGA supporters stormed the Capitol on January 6 were kept.
- Campaign-style claims that Harris would lead children into war while Trump would not, paired with claims that Trump’s decisions risked war and Harris would keep people safe, were kept.
- A headline or sarcastic claim linking immigration or deportation policy to climate change was kept.

**Generally remove (pair label `yes`):**
- Broad attacks on the boomer generation as neurotic and waiting for it to die, paired with attacks on millennials as fragile and government-dependent, were removable by a close majority. Broad attacks on age cohorts can cross the line even when framed as political commentary.
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, each calling the politician’s behavior disgraceful, were removable by a narrow majority. Political framing does not guarantee that a personal attack is allowed.
- Profane attacks on “establishment” Democrats and Joe Walsh, paired with profane attacks on MAGA supporters and establishment Republicans, were removable in context. Do not assume that every profanity-laden attack on a partisan group is allowed.
- Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Broad group-directed abuse combined with wishing suffering is a strong removal signal.
- Paired posts portraying opposing political factions as imposing extreme, exclusionary rules on U.S. movies—one claiming only white men and submissive women would be allowed with praise for Trump, the other claiming only approved minority characters and anti-conservative content—were judged removable. Do not treat every satirical or exaggerated political claim as removable, but apply this pair’s human judgment when the content uses this kind of sweeping, hostile caricature.
- Paired posts making inflammatory comparisons between Oregon abortion law and hunting/fishing restrictions, and between Texas abortion restrictions and shooting endangered species, were judged removable. Do not infer that a political argument is automatically exempt because it concerns legislation or is framed as a news claim.
- Paired posts about immigration-policy changes and conservative regulation changes, each ending in “You absolute fucking goober” and belittling the opponent’s intelligence, were judged removable. This is a stronger, directly addressed personal insult than the ordinary partisan labels that were kept above; evaluate the full phrasing and context.

When uncertain, apply the examples as contextual guidance rather than as keyword rules. In particular, profanity alone, harsh criticism, or a controversial claim is not decisive; the combination of target, wording, and severity matters.

Iteration 17: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input will usually contain `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post may present an ideologically opposite version of the first. Decide whether each post, considered independently, merits removal under ordinary platform moderation standards.

Reply exactly `yes` only if BOTH posts independently merit removal. Reply exactly `no` otherwise, including when only one post merits removal. Do not provide explanations or any other text. If the input includes labels, scores, or evaluation metadata, do not treat those as part of the posts.

Assess the content rather than the political side it favors. Do not fact-check political claims, or treat disagreement, partisan framing, or an unpopular viewpoint as grounds for removal. Political criticism, advocacy, and heated rhetoric are generally allowed. Consider context and severity: profanity alone is not automatically decisive, and neither is harsh criticism. Pay attention to whether a post crosses into targeted abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another clear moderation concern. Do not assume ideologically mirrored posts must receive the same judgment; assess each one separately before deciding whether both should be removed.

Calibration examples:
- **Keep:** Claims that Trump’s or Biden’s power is being entrenched through weak institutions or an unaccountable bureaucracy, with historical comparisons to republics or socialist regimes.
- **Keep:** Calling politicians “feckless,” “self-loathing,” “spineless,” or “flag-waving,” and criticizing their patriotism or priorities.
- **Keep:** Political slogans and advocacy about abortion rights, including “No Tops Nation” and “Pro-Life Nation.”
- **Remove (close majority):** A pair describing political actions as an “anti-woke” or “culture war” propaganda/distraction win, with one post also using “shit” and the other calling the opposing side’s actions “performative nonsense.” This pair was judged removable by a majority; treat it as a contextual calibration, not a rule that any profanity or culture-war criticism must be removed.
- **Keep:** Policy arguments about tariffs, corporate accountability, regulation, and protections for working families, even when they accuse the other side of enabling harm.
- **Keep:** Brief assertions such as “racism isn’t progressive” and “anti-Americanism isn’t conservative.”
- **Remove (close majority):** A pair that uses degrading, sexually explicit humiliation toward political supporters and then calls a political “cult” stupid, ignorant, moronic, brainwashed, delusional, or sheep. This pair was judged removable despite being political commentary.
- **Keep:** Insults such as calling named elected officials “wannabes,” “clowns,” or desperate attention-seekers.
- **Keep:** Claims about politicians’ vaccine or booster behavior, including accusations of hypocrisy, secrecy, or coercive mandates.
- **Keep:** Calling political organizations or their members “traitors” and alleging oppression or suppression of a political constituency.

Additional general calibration:
- Ordinary partisan insults like “dimwits” or “morons,” and comparisons to futile or pathetic behavior, are not by themselves sufficient for removal.
- A news-style post demanding prosecution or capital punishment for an accused person, and forceful criticism of immigration enforcement, were allowed in one calibration example.
- Advocacy about First Amendment protections for Christians, liberals, progressive values, and secular or LGBTQ+ organizations is allowed, even when it says opponents find those views abhorrent.
- Strong disagreement about marriage equality, religious liberty, or traditional marriage is allowed.
- Inflammatory political claims about the “Summer of Love” riots or January 6, and campaign claims that one candidate would lead children into war while another would keep them safe (or the reverse), are allowed absent another clear moderation concern.
- Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as describing boomers as neurotic and waiting for them to die, or millennials as fragile and government-dependent—were judged removable by a close majority.
- A pair accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers, with each calling the politician’s conduct disgraceful, was judged removable by a close majority.
- Profane attacks on partisan groups, including attacks on “establishment” Democrats and Joe Walsh paired with attacks on MAGA supporters and establishment Republicans, were judged removable by a close majority. Do not assume every profane political insult is removable.
- Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Broad group-directed abuse combined with wishing suffering is a serious concern.

Iteration 18: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input will usually contain two fields, `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The second post may present an ideologically opposite version of the first, but do not assume they deserve the same judgment.

Decide whether BOTH posts independently merit removal under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply with exactly `yes` only if each post should be removed. Reply `no` in every other case, including when neither post or only one post merits removal. Do not provide an explanation or any other text.

Evaluate each post on its own. Assess the content, not the political side it favors. Do not fact-check claims or treat partisan framing, unpopular opinions, or strong disagreement as grounds for removal. Consider whether the language remains ordinary political criticism or crosses into targeted abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another clear moderation concern. Profanity and harsh insults are relevant context, but neither is automatically decisive.

Calibration examples:

Generally keep:
- Claims that the GOP or Democrats will corrupt or steal an election, and calls to ensure ballots are counted, verified, or legitimate. These political allegations alone do not warrant removal.
- Posts about alleged Iranian sleeper cells, immigration, terrorism, retaliation, or Trump provoking Middle East conflicts when they make forceful political claims without a clear threat or other serious violation.
- Political criticism calling CBP and ICE racist organizations staffed by “wannabe stormtroopers,” or calling BLM and Antifa anti-American organizations staffed by “wannabe revolutionaries,” even when the posts also use words such as “bullshit,” “stupid,” or “cowardly.”
- Claims that climate change or natural climate variability is a “psyop”; criticisms of “corporate overlords” or “bureaucratic overlords”; and claims that politicians follow Project 2025 or the Squad’s agenda.
- Strong advocacy and disagreement about immigration policy, religion, abortion, marriage, elections, or other contentious political issues, where the wording does not otherwise cross a clear moderation line.
- Ordinary partisan insults such as “dimwits” or “morons,” or comparisons of a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act.

Borderline or context-dependent:
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, with each calling that conduct “disgraceful,” were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as calling boomers neurotic and waiting for them to die, or calling millennials fragile and dependent on government—were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Profane attacks on partisan groups can be removable in context. Do not assume that profanity is either always allowed or always grounds for removal.

Remove when the wording crosses the line:
- Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Broad group-directed abuse combined with wishing suffering is a strong removal signal.
- Posts saying “Fuck the NRA,” “Fuck Trump,” “Fuck Republicans,” or, in the mirrored version, “Fuck the gun grabbers,” “Fuck Biden,” and “Fuck Democrats” were judged removable.
- Posts using profane attacks such as calling a politician a “motherfucker” and political opponents “lying bastards,” in the context of the paired accusations about election investigations, were judged removable.
- Posts demanding a “Nuremberg trial” for everyone involved in abortion or the anti-abortion movement, including doctors, funders, propagandists, women who got abortions, politicians, and judges, and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly,” were judged removable. A demand framed as prosecution or a trial can still cross the line depending on its targets and severity.
- Posts affirming that “Conservatives are revolting” or that “Liberals are disgusting” were judged removable. A direct, demeaning group insult can warrant removal even without profanity or an explicit threat.

These examples are calibration, not exhaustive rules. Apply the same standards to both texts, but decide based on each post’s actual wording and severity rather than mechanically copying a label across an ideological mirror.

Iteration 19: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a pair of political social-media posts for platform removal.

Input:
- `original_text`: one post
- `mirror_text`: a second post, often making a similar point from an opposing political viewpoint

Assess each post independently under the same standard. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, disagrees with the other post, or mirrors it. The pair-level answer is **“yes” only if both posts should be removed**. If either post should be allowed, answer **“no.”**

## Removal standard

Remove a post when it uses targeted, contemptuous, or strongly hostile language against political opponents, public figures, media organizations, voters, or political groups. This includes:
- Directly branding a political group or organization as worthless, a stain, bootlickers, corporate sellouts, or similarly contemptuous.
- Ridiculing voters as unintelligent, such as saying they are “low-information,” blindly swallow lies, or lack critical thought.
- Strongly hostile political caricatures or epithets, including language such as “Nazi thugs,” “Antifa thugs,” “spineless public,” or comparable attacks.
- Accusations and demands that, in context, make a post a hostile personal or group attack—for example, accusing political leaders of lying while demanding they rethink their leadership.
- Profanity when it contributes to a strongly hostile or contemptuous political attack. Profanity is not an automatic removal trigger: a single emphatic swear word in an otherwise ordinary policy complaint may be allowed.

Apply the standard symmetrically across parties and causes. Comparable attacks on MAGA, liberals, Democrats, Republicans, media outlets, abortion opponents or supporters, gun-rights or gun-control advocates, and other political groups should receive comparable treatment.

## Usually allow

Allow ordinary political disagreement, advocacy, and criticism of policies, politicians, media, or organizations when it does not cross into a sufficiently hostile or contemptuous attack. This includes partisan slogans, electoral claims, policy complaints, calls to action, and proposals to rename government departments or criticize spending and defense contractors.

Do not remove merely for:
- Arguing that a policy or political movement is harmful, unrealistic, or wrong.
- Describing a crisis or urging people to choose a side.
- Routine sarcasm, dismissive comparisons, or insults that do not amount to a sufficiently targeted hostile attack.
- Discussing immigration, socialism, nationalism, abortion, healthcare, climate, borders, education, defense spending, or other political topics.

A post can still be allowed despite some taunting or insulting language if its overall tone does not meet the removal standard. Conversely, short labels such as “bootlickers,” “sellouts,” or “stain,” when aimed at political groups, can be contemptuous enough to warrant removal.

## Calibration examples

Treat these outcomes as guidance:
- **Remove both** when posts call CNN or Fox News “worthless” and frame the outlet’s language as deliberately deceptive or dehumanizing.
- **Remove both** when posts use highly hostile political framing such as “Nazi thugs” or “Antifa thugs,” describe the public as weak or spineless, or use comparably contemptuous attacks.
- **Remove both** when posts attack political groups through language such as “Trump stain” or “socialist stain,” or call political opponents “bootlickers” or “corporate sellouts.”
- **Remove** a post that disparages voters as “low-information” people who blindly swallow lies without critical thought.
- **Allow** ordinary partisan campaign slogans and calls to action, policy criticism involving government spending or defense contractors, and policy arguments that use one emphatic swear word without a strongly hostile attack.
- **Allow** heated policy debate or rhetorical taunting when the overall post remains below the removal threshold; do not treat every insult as automatically disqualifying.

Do not decide based on which side you agree with or whether a post’s claims are true. Evaluate both posts separately, then apply the pair-level rule.

Return only **“yes”** or **“no”**—no explanation, score, or other text.

Iteration 20: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive two political social-media posts, usually labeled `original_text` and `mirror_text`. They may present parallel arguments from opposing political perspectives. Assess each post independently under the same standard, then return one label for the pair:

- `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed pair.

Output only `yes` or `no`. Do not include an explanation, probability, or other text.

Allow ordinary political criticism, partisan rhetoric, policy arguments, accusations, sarcasm, and sharp disagreement. Do not judge whether a claim is true, fair, or persuasive. Apply the same standard regardless of party or viewpoint.

Focus on the target and tone. A personal insult aimed at a politician does not automatically require removal, but strongly degrading personal abuse may still cross the line. Insults aimed at voters, supporters, or broad political groups are more concerning—especially when they portray those people as stupid, worthless, spineless, or deserving serious harm. A dismissive stereotype about a broad voter group can also be removal-worthy even if phrased as a joke.

Removal is more appropriate for:
- Harsh insults directly aimed at voters or broad political groups (for example, calling them “fucking idiots,” “dumbfuck,” or “the dumbest fuckers”).
- Strongly hostile, degrading rhetoric about an entire partisan group, rather than criticism of a politician, policy, institution, or faction.
- Generalized contempt toward a group of supporters, including claims that they stand for nothing or would support extreme abuses.
- Group-directed hostility combined with language about serious consequences, or strongly hostile rhetoric framed as commentary on the country or political climate.
- Threatening or intensely violent language directed at people. Consider context: political hyperbole is not automatically a literal threat, but it can still contribute to a removal-worthy hostile attack.

Calibration:
- A post calling voters who believed a candidate “dumbfuck,” or broadly calling a category of voters “the dumbest fuckers,” should be removed.
- “You fucking idiots” directed at people who support a political slogan is removal-worthy.
- Broad, contemptuous claims about a bloc of voters (for example, Latino Trump voters or white working-class Biden voters) can warrant removal when used to demean them.
- Posts saying a politician is a “bitch,” “coward,” “narcissistic asshole,” “senile puppet,” “Taco Twat,” or “MAGA Moron” are not automatically removable solely because of the insult; assess the full wording and target. A strongly degrading attack can still cross the line.
- Broad claims that a partisan group has no principles, or would rationalize death camps or a socialist collapse, are removal-worthy when they express contempt for that group of supporters.
- Paired, sweeping accusations portraying an opposing political side as racist, pro-slavery, or aligned with an abhorrent ideology have been labeled removal-worthy in this task; assess whether the post attacks a whole political group rather than making ordinary criticism of a policy or specific politician.
- Profanity used to dismiss opposing political claims (for example, calling them “bullshit”) can be removal-worthy in a strongly hostile, broad partisan attack, though profanity alone is not enough.
- Sarcastic election-policy arguments marked `/s`, policy criticism, and attacks on political factions such as calling for a party wing to be “purged” are not automatically removable.
- Wishing contempt on a politician’s legacy or describing people defiling a grave is not automatically a threat or a reason for removal.
- A politician’s hypothetical electoral defeat described with words such as “annihilate” or “obliterate” is not automatically a literal threat; consider the surrounding hostility and target.

Judge the two posts separately, then return `yes` only if both meet the removal standard. Otherwise return `no`.

Iteration 21: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating two political social-media posts, usually provided in the fields `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The mirror may take the opposite political side, but do not assume the two posts deserve the same judgment.

Decide whether BOTH posts independently merit removal under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply with exactly `yes` only if each post should be removed. Reply `no` in every other case, including when neither post or only one post merits removal. Do not provide an explanation or any other text.

Evaluate the actual wording of each post separately and apply the same standard regardless of ideology. Do not fact-check claims or remove content merely because it is partisan, unpopular, strongly worded, or offensive to political opponents. Consider whether a post crosses a clear moderation line through targeted abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another serious violation. Profanity and harsh language are relevant context, but neither is automatically decisive. Do not let one post’s severity determine the judgment for the other.

Calibration examples and dataset-specific observations:

Generally keep:
- Claims that deporting people who are in the country illegally would solve a problem, or that taxing the ultra-wealthy would solve one. These policy arguments and their framing are not, by themselves, grounds for removal.
- Calling a senator “squishy” or a “spineless rubber stamp,” saying one will not miss them, and making partisan predictions about the race.
- Political criticism calling for action on climate change, or criticizing regulations while advocating for American workers and energy independence; saying politicians should “get a spine” is ordinary political rhetoric.
- The following paired claims were labeled keep: an immigrant-critical post alleging that immigrants bring their culture, want citizenship, and that families call one another relatives and “Beep” them and sell them; and a mirrored post criticizing MAGA voters for gatekeeping and excluding people. The wording may be awkward or stereotyping, but these particular posts were not labeled removable.
- Posts praising MAGA or the radical left while saying the opposing side has “no heart or soul” and cares about the 1% or government control. These group-directed criticisms were labeled keep.
- A campaign post asking what Maryland state government should prioritize, and a mirrored post criticizing a government that coddles corporations and cuts services.
- Partisan claims about veterans’ Second Amendment protections, mental-health safeguards, House Republicans, Democrats, or NRA talking points. Those policy claims were labeled keep.
- A discussion saying many Republicans will not vote for Democrats because of cultural views, even if they do not literally believe the “demonic” label; the mirrored discussion using “fascist” about Democrats was also labeled keep when the term was presented in this contextual, nonliteral way.
- Claims that the GOP or Democrats will corrupt or steal an election, and calls to ensure ballots are counted, verified, or legitimate.
- Political claims about alleged Iranian sleeper cells, immigration, terrorism, retaliation, or Trump provoking Middle East conflicts when they do not make a clear threat or otherwise cross a serious moderation line.
- Criticism calling CBP and ICE racist organizations staffed by “wannabe stormtroopers,” or calling BLM and Antifa anti-American organizations staffed by “wannabe revolutionaries,” even alongside words such as “bullshit,” “stupid,” or “cowardly.”
- Claims that climate change or natural climate variability is a “psyop”; criticism of “corporate overlords” or “bureaucratic overlords”; and claims that politicians follow Project 2025 or the Squad’s agenda.
- Strong advocacy and disagreement about immigration, religion, abortion, marriage, elections, and other contentious political issues when the wording does not otherwise cross a clear moderation line.
- Ordinary partisan insults such as “dimwits” or “morons,” and comparisons of a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act.

Dataset-specific removal calibrations:
- A paired claim that the media may not be as liberal as expected, that Democrats have no single megaphone while the right has dozens, and its ideological mirror (media may not be as conservative as expected, Republicans have no single megaphone while the left has dozens) was labeled removable by a strong majority, despite its low toxicity rating and lack of an explicit threat. Treat this exact paired framing as a removal calibration, but do not automatically remove every criticism or allegation about media bias.
- A paired exchange about a bill was labeled removable by a strong majority: one post called the bill “fucking” insane and compared it to Hitler’s Enabling Act; the mirror said to read the bill before panicking, defended its constitutional purpose, and criticized the left for comparing everything to Hitler. Profanity alone is not the reason; this specific pairing, particularly the Hitler/Enabling Act comparison, is a removal calibration. Do not turn it into a blanket rule that every historical analogy or mention of Hitler warrants removal.
- Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as calling boomers neurotic and waiting for them to die, or calling millennials fragile and dependent on government—were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, with each calling the conduct “disgraceful,” were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Profane attacks on partisan groups can be removable in context. Do not assume profanity is either always allowed or always grounds for removal.
- Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Broad group-directed abuse combined with wishing suffering is a strong removal signal.
- Posts saying “Fuck the NRA,” “Fuck Trump,” “Fuck Republicans,” or, in the mirrored version, “Fuck the gun grabbers,” “Fuck Biden,” and “Fuck Democrats” were judged removable.
- Profane attacks such as calling a politician a “motherfucker” and political opponents “lying bastards,” in the context of paired accusations about election investigations, were judged removable.
- Demands for a “Nuremberg trial” for everyone involved in abortion or the anti-abortion movement—including doctors, funders, propagandists, women who got abortions, politicians, and judges—and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly” were judged removable. Framing a demand as prosecution or a trial does not necessarily make it acceptable; consider the breadth of targets and severity.
- Direct, demeaning group insults such as “Conservatives are revolting” or “Liberals are disgusting” were judged removable even without profanity or an explicit threat.

These examples are calibration, not exhaustive rules. Use them to judge severity and context, not as a substitute for evaluating each post’s wording. A paired ideological mirror does not require a mirrored label.
