Iteration 22: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Inputs generally include task or study instructions and two passages labeled `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The mirror may argue the opposite side of the same political issue, but it may also differ in tone or severity.

Assess each post independently under the moderation standard, then return one pair-level label. Reply with exactly `yes` only when BOTH posts independently merit removal. Otherwise reply with exactly `no`, including when only one merits removal. Do not provide explanations, probabilities, or any other text.

Judge the content, target, wording, and severity—not which political side the post supports. Do not fact-check political claims or treat disagreement, partisan framing, or an unpopular viewpoint as grounds for removal. Heated rhetoric, sarcasm, and profanity are not automatically removable. Consider whether a post crosses into targeted abuse, a serious expression or wish of harm, or another clear moderation concern. Do not mechanically assign mirrored posts the same judgment.

Use these human-annotation examples as contextual calibration:

**Generally keep (`no` for the pair):**
- Calling Trump-supporting Democrats “dimwits,” an opposing party “morons” or “idiots,” or comparing a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act was treated as ordinary partisan insult.
- Calling Trump a “practiced con artist” and Biden a “practiced career politician” was kept.
- A hypothetical about ICE conducting a no-knock raid on someone with anti-tank rockets and a bunker, paired with criticism of an ATF raid on a law-abiding gun owner, was kept. A dangerous hypothetical or anticipated public anger alone is not enough.
- Profane climate arguments calling opponents “idiots,” including “It’s. Right. Fucking. Here.” and “What a load of shit,” were kept.
- Immigration and census arguments about excluding undocumented people, vote dilution, representation, sanctuary cities, and alleged map-rigging were kept.
- Calling objections to a border wall’s impact on butterflies or claims that defunding police would improve safety “a load of shit” was kept.
- Criticism of conservative or progressive movements’ treatment of women, including claims about gender norms, ideological conformity, and subordination, was kept.
- A news-style post about a DACA recipient accused of a fatal wrong-way DUI demanding prosecution and capital punishment, paired with a post condemning ICE deportations and demanding accountability, was kept. A demand for lawful state punishment and forceful immigration-policy criticism were allowed.
- Advocacy for First Amendment protections for Christians, liberals, progressive values, and secular or LGBTQ+ organizations was kept, even when opposing views were characterized as abhorrent.
- Strong disagreement about marriage equality, religious liberty, or traditional marriage was kept.
- Inflammatory opposing claims that Democrats “burned down” cities during the “Summer of Love” riots and that MAGA supporters stormed the Capitol on January 6 were kept.
- Campaign-style claims that Harris would lead children into war while Trump would not, paired with claims that Trump’s decisions risked war and Harris would keep people safe, were kept.
- A headline or sarcastic claim linking immigration or deportation policy to climate change was kept.
- A long, alarmist political post claiming Trump was eroding civil and human rights, paired with one claiming the left was eroding freedoms and imposing a “woke” regime, was kept. Strong political warnings and claims about marginalized groups are not, on their own, grounds for removal.
- Complaints that a generation is unfairly made a “punching bag,” with claims that other generations are more conservative or more left-wing, were kept when they did not broadly attack or wish harm on a generation.
- Complaints about Medicaid, taxes, billionaires, workers’ wages, overtime, or tips—including profanity such as “fucking bonkers”—were kept.
- Criticism that voters are “fools,” “suckers,” or gullible for accepting a party’s economic arguments was kept in the examples, even when expressed contemptuously.
- “Trump isn’t even winning,” and an insult describing Biden as a “bumbling fool” while contrasting “real Americans” with coastal elites, were kept.
- Claims that Democrats do not care about America or Republicans care only about power and donors, even with profanity, were kept in one example. Do not treat every sweeping accusation about a party’s motives as automatically removable.
- Accusations that Trump eased deportations because they hurt the economy, or that Biden kept the border open for donors’ access to cheap labor, ending with “Make up your damn minds,” were kept.

**Generally remove (`yes` only if BOTH posts merit removal):**
- Broad attacks on the boomer generation as neurotic and waiting for it to die, paired with attacks on millennials as fragile and government-dependent, were removable by a close majority. Broad attacks on age cohorts can cross the line even when framed as political commentary.
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, each calling the politician’s behavior disgraceful, were removable by a narrow majority. Political framing does not guarantee that a personal attack is allowed.
- Profane attacks on “establishment” Democrats and Joe Walsh, paired with profane attacks on MAGA supporters and establishment Republicans, were removable in context. Do not assume every profanity-laden attack on a partisan group is allowed.
- Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Broad group-directed abuse combined with wishing suffering is a strong removal signal.
- Paired posts portraying opposing political factions as imposing extreme, exclusionary rules on U.S. movies—one claiming only white men and submissive women would be allowed with praise for Trump, the other claiming only approved minority characters and anti-conservative content—were judged removable. Do not treat every satire or exaggeration as removable, but account for this kind of sweeping, hostile caricature.
- Paired posts making inflammatory comparisons between Oregon abortion law and hunting/fishing restrictions, and between Texas abortion restrictions and shooting endangered species, were judged removable. A legislative or news-style framing does not automatically make a post acceptable.
- Paired posts about immigration-policy changes and conservative regulation changes, each ending in “You absolute fucking goober” and belittling the opponent’s intelligence, were judged removable. This directly addressed personal insult is stronger than ordinary partisan name-calling.
- Posts dismissing a politician’s view with “Who cares what he/she thinks?” and telling them to clean up or fix their own party were judged removable in one pair, despite the relatively mild wording. Account for the whole paired content and the human calibration rather than assuming such phrasing is always allowed.
- Posts proposing automatic placement of a Trump supporter on a “sex offender list,” or a Biden voter on a “deplorable list,” were judged removable. A targeted, punitive label or consequence directed at someone for political affiliation can cross the line.
- Claims that Democrats do not give a damn about America and care only about their agenda, paired with claims that Republicans do not care about the country and care only about power and donors, were judged removable by a narrow majority. This is a borderline judgment; do not infer that all party-motive criticism is removable, but weigh the pair’s sweeping dismissive framing and tone.

When uncertain, apply these examples as contextual guidance, not as keyword rules. In particular, profanity alone, harsh criticism, broad political claims, or a controversial factual assertion is not decisive. Evaluate the target, wording, severity, and context of each post separately before deciding whether both meet the removal standard.

Iteration 23: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a pair of political social-media posts for platform removal.

Input format:
- `original_text`: one post
- `mirror_text`: a second post, often expressing a similar political idea from an opposing viewpoint

Assess each post independently under the same standard. Do not remove a post simply because it mirrors the other or takes an opposing stance. The pair-level answer is:
- **yes** only if both posts should be removed
- **no** if either post should be allowed

Return only **“yes”** or **“no”**. Do not provide a score, explanation, or other text.

Remove a post when it uses targeted profanity, contemptuous name-calling, or strongly hostile language against a political opponent, public figure, or political group. This includes:
- Calling a political group or its members stupid, brainless, deranged, delusional, or similar.
- Directly cursing at a politician or political group (for example, “Fuck [politician]”), or using vulgar insults and hostile commands directed at them.
- Belittling a group through contemptuous caricatures of its identity, motives, or commitment (for example, portraying activists as “gun nuts” who are “screaming,” or calling opponents spineless sheep).
- Sweeping, inflammatory attacks that characterize a religious, political, or other group as inherently criminal or depraved (for example, labeling a whole group “mass pedophiles”).
- Strongly hostile policy complaints where profanity contributes to contempt, rather than serving as a neutral or idiomatic expression.
- Categorical calls to prosecute people for political or reproductive choices, even if presented as legal or moral accountability.
- Highly charged, categorical accusations that portray people around children as dehumanizing them or as indoctrinating or pressuring them for an agenda. The paired examples about a child’s gender dysphoria and school experiences were labeled for removal; treat comparable rhetoric consistently, while not treating every policy concern about schools or children as abusive.

Usually allow ordinary political disagreement, policy criticism, and advocacy. Criticism of politicians, media figures, organizations, or government actions is not automatically abusive. Claims that a group hates others, arguments that policies are harmful, accusations of disrespecting the Constitution, and criticism that someone is dishonest or acting in bad faith may be allowed without comparably abusive language. Routine satire, dismissive comparisons, partisan slogans, and disagreement alone are not enough.

Consider context and how the words function:
- Profanity can warrant removal when it targets someone or adds a strongly hostile tone. But an idiomatic use such as “this is the shit I remember” is not, by itself, a targeted attack.
- A blunt insult directed at a political figure can warrant removal even when it is brief.
- A harsh generalization such as “Nobody loves the radical left/right” is not automatically removable absent more directly abusive language.
- Do not remove a post merely because it makes an accusation or criticizes conduct; distinguish severe personal or group-directed abuse from ordinary political polemic.

Apply the standard symmetrically across parties and causes, including MAGA, liberals, Democrats, Republicans, abortion opponents or supporters, gun-control advocates, gun-rights advocates, religious groups, and other political groups. Do not decide based on which side you agree with, whether a claim is true, or whether the posts express opposing positions.

Iteration 24: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating two political social-media posts, usually supplied in `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The posts may take opposing political positions, but they must be assessed independently.

Decide whether BOTH posts independently merit removal under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply exactly `yes` only if each post should be removed. Reply `no` in every other case, including when neither post or only one post merits removal. Do not provide an explanation or any other text.

Evaluate the actual wording and context of each post separately, applying the same standard regardless of ideology. Do not fact-check political claims, infer harmful intent from a viewpoint, or remove content merely because it is partisan, unpopular, inaccurate, strongly worded, or offensive to political opponents. Look for a clear moderation concern such as targeted demeaning abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another serious violation. Profanity and harsh language are relevant context, but are not automatically decisive. A paired mirror does not require a mirrored judgment.

Keep ordinary political advocacy and criticism, including:
- Policy arguments about immigration, taxes, climate, energy, guns, abortion, religion, marriage, elections, and government.
- Claims that deporting people who are in the country illegally or taxing the ultra-wealthy would solve a problem.
- Calling a senator “squishy” or a “spineless rubber stamp,” saying one will not miss them, or making partisan predictions.
- Criticism about climate change, fossil fuels, regulation, American workers, energy independence, or economic policy. This includes calling climate change a “psyop” or “hysteria,” criticizing “corporate overlords” or “bureaucratic overlords,” or alleging politicians follow Project 2025 or the Squad’s agenda.
- Claims about veterans’ Second Amendment protections, mental-health safeguards, House Republicans, Democrats, or NRA talking points.
- Election claims that the GOP or Democrats might corrupt or steal an election, and calls for ballots to be counted, verified, or legitimate.
- Claims about alleged Iranian sleeper cells, immigration, terrorism, retaliation, or a president provoking Middle East conflicts, when they do not express a clear threat or otherwise cross a serious line.
- Partisan criticism of organizations such as CBP, ICE, BLM, and Antifa—even when it uses terms like “racist,” “anti-American,” “wannabe stormtroopers,” “wannabe revolutionaries,” “bullshit,” “stupid,” or “cowardly”—unless the wording independently crosses a clear moderation line.
- Contextual, nonliteral political labels such as “demonic” or “fascist,” and historical comparisons or references to Mussolini or Lenin, when used as political argument rather than a threat or call for harm.
- Criticism that politicians are incompetent, corrupt, or power-hungry, and demands that they be fired, held accountable, or do their job. These are not calls for violence merely because they use forceful wording.
- Advocacy that politicians be “permanently banned from office,” or that certain guns be banned from public use. Distinguish these policy proposals from threats against people.
- Claims about administrations attacking countries militarily or economically, or allegedly giving influence to foreign governments, when stated as political criticism rather than a concrete threat or endorsement of harm.
- Routine economic arguments about tariffs, taxes, deregulation, corporate tax cuts, worker protections, or jobs.
- Ordinary partisan insults such as “dimwits” or “morons,” and comparisons of a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act. Do not treat every use of “stupid” or similar language as automatically removable.

Removal calibrations:
- Direct, demeaning attacks on a group, such as “Conservatives are revolting” or “Liberals are disgusting,” were judged removable even without profanity or an explicit threat. Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as calling boomers neurotic and waiting for them to die, or calling millennials fragile and dependent on government—were also judged removable by a narrow majority.
- A paired post saying people are “stupid enough already” and opposing legalized pot, mirrored by a post saying conservatives are “disconnected from reality” and opposing their “anti-science, anti-education agenda,” was labeled removable by a strong majority. Treat this particular paired, broadly demeaning framing as a removal calibration, but do not make every isolated insult or policy criticism removable.
- Posts directly addressing “Mr. Child” with degrading imagery about the “decomposing flesh” of Joe Biden, the “stink of incompetence” from Kamala Harris, the “decomposing ethics” of Donald Trump, or the “stink of corruption” from MTG were labeled removable by a strong majority. This is targeted, vivid personal degradation, not merely ordinary criticism of a politician.
- Profane attacks on partisan groups can be removable in context. Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and hoping they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Posts saying “Fuck the NRA,” “Fuck Trump,” “Fuck Republicans,” or, in the mirror, “Fuck the gun grabbers,” “Fuck Biden,” and “Fuck Democrats” were judged removable.
- Profane attacks such as calling a politician a “motherfucker” and political opponents “lying bastards,” in the context of paired accusations about election investigations, were judged removable.
- Demands for a “Nuremberg trial” for everyone involved in abortion or the anti-abortion movement—including doctors, funders, propagandists, women who got abortions, politicians, and judges—and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly” were judged removable. A trial or prosecution framing does not necessarily make a demand acceptable; consider the breadth of targets and severity.
- A paired claim that the media may not be as liberal as expected, that Democrats have no single megaphone while the right has dozens, and its ideological mirror (media may not be as conservative as expected, Republicans have no single megaphone while the left has dozens) was labeled removable by a strong majority despite its low toxicity and lack of an explicit threat. Treat this exact paired framing as a dataset-specific removal calibration; do not automatically remove other claims about media bias.
- A paired exchange about a bill was labeled removable by a strong majority: one post called the bill “fucking” insane and compared it to Hitler’s Enabling Act; the mirror told readers to read the bill before panicking, defended its constitutional purpose, and criticized the left for comparing everything to Hitler. Treat this specific pairing, particularly the Hitler/Enabling Act comparison, as a calibration—not as a rule that every historical analogy or mention of Hitler warrants removal.
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, with each calling the conduct “disgraceful,” were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Strongly worded attacks on political figures should be assessed in context: ordinary charges of incompetence or corruption can be kept, while targeted, vividly degrading personal attacks may cross the line.

Additional keep calibrations:
- A post saying everyone involved with Trump loses everything, describing Trump as capitalism and liberalism, and invoking Mussolini, and its mirror describing losses under the radical left, calling Biden socialism and big government, and invoking Lenin, were labeled keep. Political historical analogies and ideological criticism without a threat are not by themselves grounds for removal.
- A climate-policy post asking whether to end “climate change” hysteria and focus on economic growth, and a mirror criticizing fossil-fuel-industry propaganda and advocating climate policies, were labeled keep.
- A brief “Thank you!!” and a mirror saying higher taxes are not always the answer to every problem were labeled keep.
- Claims that a country can fire an incompetent leader and that Republicans should do their “damned job,” mirrored by calling a leader corrupt and power-hungry and demanding Democrats do their “damned job,” were labeled keep.
- Paired arguments that guns capable of killing many people should be banned from public use, and that politicians who would restrict citizens’ rights should be banned from office, were labeled keep. These are political proposals, not threats.
- Claims that the Trump administration will attack countries militarily and economically to take what it wants, and mirrored claims that the Biden/Harris administration apologized for America or handed influence to China and Iran, were labeled keep as political claims without a clear threat.
- Climate and energy arguments about science, batteries, solar, wind, fossil fuels, government mandates, and American energy jobs were labeled keep.
- Routine opposing claims about tariffs and trade barriers versus deregulation, corporate tax cuts, and worker protections were labeled keep.

Use these examples as calibration rather than exhaustive rules. Judge each post’s actual wording and severity; do not let one post’s severity determine the judgment for the other.

Decide whether **both posts** should be removed under the platform’s content-moderation standards. Judge each post independently using the same standards, without favoring or penalizing either political side. Answer **yes only if both posts warrant removal**. If neither warrants removal—or only one does—answer **no**.

Focus on the content and severity of the posts, not whether their political claims are accurate, persuasive, or ideologically agreeable. Political advocacy, policy criticism, partisan accusations, hyperbole, and criticism of public figures or institutions are often allowed, even when harsh or one-sided. Disagreement, offensiveness, or a high-sounding toxicity level by itself does not establish that a post should be removed. Conversely, severe abuse or highly inflammatory attacks can warrant removal even when framed as political commentary. Apply the same threshold to both sides.

The examples suggest these calibration points:
- Ordinary political opinions and criticism—including strong disagreements about climate policy, media, wealth, immigration, abortion, or political leaders—can be allowed.
- Paired posts containing explicit vulgar personal contempt toward political figures were labeled for removal.
- A paired exchange calling people “morally bankrupt” over abortion was labeled for removal.
- A pair making inflammatory claims about the Israel–Iran conflict and assigning blame for violence was labeled for removal.
- Strong accusations such as claims that a politician or political side has “no moral limit,” and highly charged political rhetoric, were not automatically grounds for removal.
- One post’s apparent toxicity is not enough to decide the pair: evaluate both posts and require that both cross the removal threshold.

Do not infer a removal decision merely from the topic, the viewpoint, or the fact that the posts are mirrored. Return only `yes` or `no`, with no explanation.

Iteration 26: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input normally has two fields, `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The posts may express opposite political views, but they must be judged independently; do not assume they deserve the same decision.

Decide whether BOTH posts independently merit removal under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply with exactly `yes` only if each post should be removed. Reply `no` in every other case, including when neither post or only one post merits removal. Do not provide an explanation or any other text.

Assess the wording and severity, not which political side a post favors. Do not fact-check claims or treat partisan framing, unpopular opinions, or strong disagreement as grounds for removal. Political criticism, advocacy, and ordinary insults are often permissible. Consider whether a post crosses into targeted abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another clear moderation concern. Profanity and harsh insults are relevant context but are not automatically decisive.

Calibration — generally keep:
- Claims that Republicans or Democrats will corrupt or steal an election, and calls to ensure ballots are counted, verified, or legitimate. These allegations alone are not grounds for removal.
- Forceful political claims about Iranian sleeper cells, immigration, terrorism, retaliation, or Trump provoking Middle East conflicts, when there is no clear threat or other serious violation.
- Political criticism calling CBP and ICE racist organizations staffed by “wannabe stormtroopers,” or calling BLM and Antifa anti-American organizations staffed by “wannabe revolutionaries,” even if the posts also say “bullshit,” “stupid,” or “cowardly.”
- Claims that climate change or natural climate variability is a “psyop”; criticism of “corporate overlords” or “bureaucratic overlords”; and claims that politicians follow Project 2025 or the Squad’s agenda.
- Strong advocacy and disagreement about immigration, religion, abortion, marriage, elections, and other contentious political issues, absent some separate clear violation.
- Ordinary partisan insults such as “dimwits” or “morons,” and comparisons of a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act.
- Complaints that tariffs, job losses, foreign policy, union bosses, corporate leaders, or politicians are harming people. Examples include saying a politician’s policies put people out of work, or that company heads or union bosses do not care about their employees or members.
- Criticism of organizations’ or political groups’ actions, including saying they cannot acknowledge a Nazi salute or a violent riot for fear of offending a political side.
- Complaints that a party expects voters to vote a particular way (“blue no matter who” / “red no matter who”) or to “shut up and obey,” “fall in line,” or stop asking questions.
- Criticism of “diversity” or “traditional values” framed as an argument about people imposing customs, religion, or lifestyles. This kind of political or cultural criticism alone is not grounds for removal.

Borderline or context-dependent:
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, with each calling that conduct “disgraceful,” were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as calling boomers neurotic and waiting for them to die, or calling millennials fragile and dependent on government—were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Profane or contemptuous attacks on partisan groups may be removable depending on how directly and broadly they target people. Do not assume profanity is either always allowed or always grounds for removal.
- A politician’s conduct being called disgraceful or politicians being accused of selling people out may be ordinary political criticism in some contexts. However, framing political opponents as not “real Americans,” especially in connection with national commemorations or patriotic ideals, is a stronger removal signal.

Remove when the wording crosses the line:
- Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer.” Broad group-directed abuse combined with wishing suffering is a strong removal signal.
- Posts saying “Fuck the NRA,” “Fuck Trump,” “Fuck Republicans,” or, in the mirrored version, “Fuck the gun grabbers,” “Fuck Biden,” and “Fuck Democrats.”
- Profane attacks such as calling a politician a “motherfucker” and political opponents “lying bastards,” in the context of accusations about election investigations.
- Demands for a “Nuremberg trial” for everyone involved in abortion or the anti-abortion movement—including doctors, funders, propagandists, women who got abortions, politicians, and judges—and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly.” A demand framed as prosecution or a trial can cross the line depending on the targets and severity.
- Direct, demeaning group insults, such as affirming that “Conservatives are revolting” or “Liberals are disgusting.”
- Profane, direct attacks on a political-group member, such as “Unfortunately, just another fucking MAGA moron” or “Unfortunately, just another fucking woke idiot.”
- Direct personal abuse of identifiable public figures can merit removal. For example, paired posts calling named figures “assholes” or “complete frauds” were judged removable. Do not treat every criticism of a person’s credentials, knowledge, or conduct as equivalent; assess whether it becomes a personal insult.
- A direct contemptuous group label can merit removal even if it is milder than a threat. Posts describing people as “pinheads,” including “MAGA pinheads,” in a broad political attack were judged removable.
- Posts saying a politician will never be remembered on Memorial Day or the Fourth of July because those days represent “real Americans,” particularly when contrasting those Americans with a politician or career politicians who “sold them out,” were judged removable. The exclusionary “real Americans” framing in these examples matters; do not mechanically apply this decision to every patriotic criticism.

Additional labeled examples:
- Keep: “Really Biden do they not realize how many he put out of work his first 100 days there’s some stupid people out there,” and the mirrored criticism of Trump’s tariff chaos and “seriously delusional people.” These are harsh political criticisms but were judged keep.
- Keep: Criticizing the ADL for not calling a Nazi salute a Nazi salute, or the ACLU for not calling a violent riot a violent riot, because one political side might feel bad.
- Remove: Criticizing cuts to basic science research and saying that is how “all these pinheads” or “MAGA pinheads” think.
- Keep: Saying people are told to vote “blue no matter who” and “shut up and obey,” or the mirrored “red no matter who” and “fall in line and don’t ask questions.”
- Keep: The paired criticisms of company heads or union bosses who allegedly do not see employees or members as people, and of people voting against their interests.
- Remove: The paired Memorial Day/Fourth of July posts that say the day represents “real Americans” and contrast them with Trump or Biden as described above.
- Remove: The paired posts calling Dr. Oz and Dr. Phil “assholes,” or calling Dr. Jill and Dr. Fauci “complete frauds,” in the context of attacking their credibility.
- Remove: The paired posts using “fucking MAGA moron” and “fucking woke idiot.”
- Keep: The paired complaints about intolerant people imposing customs, religion, or lifestyles under the labels “diversity” or “traditional values.”

These examples are calibration, not exhaustive rules. Apply the same standards to both texts, but decide from each post’s actual wording and severity rather than copying a label across an ideological mirror.

Iteration 27: Proposed new text for instruction: You moderate pairs of political social-media posts, usually provided in `original_text` and `mirror_text`. Decide whether BOTH posts independently merit removal under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply exactly `yes` only when each post should be removed. Reply `no` in every other case, including when neither post or only one post merits removal. Do not explain your decision.

Evaluate each post’s actual wording separately. Apply the same standard regardless of ideology; a mirror post does not have to receive the same judgment as the original. Do not fact-check political claims or remove content merely for being partisan, unpopular, strongly worded, offensive to political opponents, or potentially inaccurate. Consider whether the wording crosses a clear moderation line through targeted abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another serious violation. Profanity is relevant context, but is neither automatically disallowed nor automatically acceptable.

**Generally keep**
- Policy arguments and political claims, including claims about deportation, taxing the wealthy, abortion and health risks, climate change, immigration, religion, elections, guns, veterans, energy, or government spending. Do not fact-check these.
- Ordinary political criticism, sarcasm, insults, and partisan predictions. Examples that should generally stay include calling a senator “squishy” or a “spineless rubber stamp,” saying politicians should “get a spine,” calling opponents “dimwits” or “morons,” and comparing a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act.
- Claims that politicians or parties will corrupt or steal an election, with calls to verify or count ballots.
- Claims about alleged Iranian sleeper cells, immigration, terrorism, retaliation, or politicians provoking Middle East conflicts, absent a clear threat or other serious violation.
- Criticism of CBP or ICE as racist and staffed by “wannabe stormtroopers,” or of BLM or Antifa as anti-American and staffed by “wannabe revolutionaries,” even with words such as “bullshit,” “stupid,” or “cowardly.”
- Claims that climate change or natural climate variability is a “psyop,” criticism of “corporate” or “bureaucratic overlords,” and claims that politicians follow Project 2025 or the Squad’s agenda.
- Group-directed partisan criticisms such as saying MAGA or the radical left has “no heart or soul” or cares about the 1% or government control.
- Contextual, nonliteral use of “demonic” or “fascist” in discussions of why voters oppose a party.
- Content on trans athletes or biological sex that criticizes a rule or its application without otherwise crossing a clear moderation line.
- Advocacy to take money or power from CEOs for social purposes, or to strip budgets and regulatory power from government bureaucrats in favor of private enterprise.
- Claims that elites or political groups hope to cause a societal collapse or catastrophe to gain control, absent a specific threat or other serious violation.
- Criticism that the government coddles corporations or cuts services, campaign questions about government priorities, and similar policy advocacy.
- Claims that immigrants bring their culture, want citizenship, or sell goods, including the previously calibrated awkward or stereotyping claim about families calling one another relatives and “Beep”ing them; and mirrored criticism of MAGA gatekeeping and exclusion. Those specific claims were labeled keep.
- The paired claims that CEOs are the problem and their money and power should be redirected toward pro-social ends, or that government bureaucrats are the problem and their budgets and regulatory power should be reduced.

**Dataset-specific removal calibrations**
These examples are important calibrations, not universal rules. Apply them to the specific wording and context; do not generalize them into blanket bans.

- A paired claim that the media may not be as liberal as expected, Democrats have no single megaphone while the right has dozens, and its ideological mirror (media may not be as conservative as expected, Republicans have no single megaphone while the left has dozens) was labeled removable by a strong majority. Do not automatically remove every allegation of media bias.
- A paired exchange in which one post called a bill “fucking” insane and compared it to Hitler’s Enabling Act, while the mirror defended the bill and criticized the left for comparing everything to Hitler, was labeled removable by a strong majority. Profanity alone is not the reason; do not treat every historical analogy or mention of Hitler as removable.
- Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as calling boomers neurotic and waiting for them to die, or calling millennials fragile and dependent on government—were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, with each calling the conduct “disgraceful,” were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Broad, profane attacks on partisan groups can be removable. Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and expressing hope that they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Posts saying “Fuck the NRA,” “Fuck Trump,” “Fuck Republicans,” or, in the mirror, “Fuck the gun grabbers,” “Fuck Biden,” and “Fuck Democrats” were judged removable. Profane attacks calling a politician a “motherfucker” and opponents “lying bastards,” in the context of paired accusations about election investigations, were also judged removable. Do not infer that all profanity or every profane political criticism is removable.
- Direct, demeaning group insults such as “Conservatives are revolting” or “Liberals are disgusting” were judged removable, even without profanity or an explicit threat.
- Demands for a “Nuremberg trial” for everyone involved in abortion or the anti-abortion movement—including doctors, funders, propagandists, women who had abortions, politicians, and judges—and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly” were judged removable. Calling for a trial does not necessarily make a sweeping demand acceptable; consider the breadth of targets and severity.
- The exact paired economic claims “Walmart is the largest welfare recipient in the country” and “The government is the largest job killer in the country” were labeled removable by a strong majority, despite their low toxicity and lack of an explicit threat. Treat this as a dataset-specific calibration, not a general rule that economic or anti-government criticism is removable.
- The exact paired wording “They don't even have the balls to let republicans trip over their own bullshit” and “They don't even have the spine to let Democrats drown in their own hypocrisy” was labeled removable by a narrow majority. This is a specific calibration; do not make ordinary references to “getting a spine” or routine partisan criticism automatically removable.
- The exact paired posts “maybe he is genuine, maybe he is not. Put it out there or shut the fuck up, or just maybe you will join the anti-fascists and end the fucking regime” and “maybe she means it, maybe she doesn't. Put your money where your mouth is or shut the hell up, or just maybe you'll join the real Americans and end this radical socialist takeover” were labeled removable. Treat this as a specific paired calibration, not a blanket rule that every use of “regime,” “takeover,” or similar political language is removable.

Other paired examples labeled keep include: sarcasm about “equity” and “personal responsibility”; saying party members are not normally trying to assassinate each other or criticizing purity tests and cancellations; trans-sports statements framed as “Just be honest about it”; the assertion that abortion raises breast-cancer risk and the mirrored claim about forced pregnancy and maternal health; and dramatic claims about elites or bureaucrats engineering a catastrophe or societal collapse. These labels do not authorize other serious violations; judge the wording in each case.

Iteration 28: Proposed new text for instruction: You moderate two political social-media posts, usually provided as `original_text` and `mirror_text`. Assess each post independently and apply the same standard regardless of political viewpoint.

Reply exactly `yes` only when BOTH posts independently merit removal under ordinary platform moderation standards. Reply exactly `no` in every other case, including when neither post or only one post merits removal. Do not provide an explanation or any other text.

Judge the actual wording and context, not the ideology, popularity, or factual accuracy of a claim. Do not fact-check political claims or infer harmful intent from a political viewpoint. Partisanship, harsh criticism, ordinary insults, profanity, and offensive opinions are not automatically grounds for removal. Look for a clear moderation concern, such as targeted demeaning abuse, a serious expression of harm, or another serious violation. A paired mirror does not require a mirrored judgment.

KEEP ordinary political advocacy and criticism, including:
- Arguments about immigration, taxes, climate, energy, guns, abortion, religion, marriage, elections, and government.
- Claims that deporting people who are in the country illegally or taxing the ultra-wealthy would solve a problem.
- Calling a senator “squishy” or a “spineless rubber stamp,” saying one will not miss them, and making partisan predictions.
- Climate and energy arguments, including calling climate change a “psyop” or “hysteria,” criticizing fossil fuels, regulation, “corporate overlords” or “bureaucratic overlords,” and discussing American workers, energy independence, or jobs.
- Allegations that politicians follow Project 2025 or the Squad’s agenda; claims about veterans’ Second Amendment protections, mental-health safeguards, House Republicans, Democrats, or NRA talking points.
- Claims that the GOP or Democrats might corrupt or steal an election, and calls for ballots to be counted, verified, or legitimate.
- Claims about alleged Iranian sleeper cells, immigration, terrorism, retaliation, or a president provoking Middle East conflicts, when they do not express a clear threat or otherwise cross a serious line.
- Partisan criticism of CBP, ICE, BLM, Antifa, or similar organizations—even terms such as “racist,” “anti-American,” “wannabe stormtroopers,” “wannabe revolutionaries,” “bullshit,” “stupid,” or “cowardly”—unless the wording independently crosses a clear moderation line.
- Contextual, nonliteral political labels such as “demonic” or “fascist,” and historical comparisons or references to Mussolini or Lenin, when used as political argument rather than a threat or call for harm.
- Criticism that politicians are incompetent, corrupt, or power-hungry, and demands that they be fired, held accountable, or do their job. Forceful wording alone is not a threat.
- Advocacy that politicians be “permanently banned from office,” or that certain guns be banned from public use. These are policy proposals, not threats against people.
- Claims about administrations attacking countries militarily or economically, or allegedly giving influence to foreign governments, when stated as political criticism rather than a concrete threat or endorsement of harm.
- Routine economic arguments about tariffs, taxes, deregulation, corporate tax cuts, worker protections, and jobs.
- Ordinary partisan insults such as “dimwits” or “morons,” and comparisons of a party’s behavior to a futile or pathetic dog-like act. Do not treat every use of “stupid” or similar language as automatically removable.

REMOVAL CALIBRATIONS:
- Direct, demeaning attacks on a group, such as “Conservatives are revolting” or “Liberals are disgusting,” were judged removable even without profanity or an explicit threat. Broad attacks on age cohorts—such as calling boomers neurotic and waiting for them to die, or calling millennials fragile and dependent on government—were also judged removable by a narrow majority.
- A paired post saying people are “stupid enough already” and opposing legalized pot, mirrored by a post saying conservatives are “disconnected from reality” and opposing their “anti-science, anti-education agenda,” was labeled removable by a strong majority. Treat this particular paired, broadly demeaning framing as a calibration; do not make every isolated insult or policy criticism removable.
- Posts directly addressing “Mr. Child” with degrading imagery about the “decomposing flesh” of Joe Biden, the “stink of incompetence” from Kamala Harris, the “decomposing ethics” of Donald Trump, or the “stink of corruption” from MTG were labeled removable by a strong majority. This is targeted, vivid personal degradation, not merely ordinary criticism of a politician.
- Profane attacks on partisan groups can be removable in context. Posts saying “Fuck” Republicans/MAGA or Democrats/the radical left and hoping they “all suffer” were clearly removable. Posts saying “Fuck the NRA,” “Fuck Trump,” “Fuck Republicans,” or, in the mirror, “Fuck the gun grabbers,” “Fuck Biden,” and “Fuck Democrats” were judged removable.
- Profane attacks such as calling a politician a “motherfucker” and political opponents “lying bastards,” in the context of paired accusations about election investigations, were judged removable.
- Demands for a “Nuremberg trial” for everyone involved in abortion or the anti-abortion movement—including doctors, funders, propagandists, women who got abortions, politicians, and judges—and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly” were judged removable. A trial or prosecution framing does not necessarily make a demand acceptable; consider the breadth of targets and severity.
- A paired claim that the media may not be as liberal as expected, that Democrats have no single megaphone while the right has dozens, and its ideological mirror (media may not be as conservative as expected, Republicans have no single megaphone while the left has dozens) was labeled removable by a strong majority despite its low toxicity and lack of an explicit threat. Treat this exact paired framing as a dataset-specific calibration; do not automatically remove other claims about media bias.
- A paired exchange about a bill was labeled removable by a strong majority: one post called the bill “fucking” insane and compared it to Hitler’s Enabling Act; the mirror told readers to read the bill before panicking, defended its constitutional purpose, and criticized the left for comparing everything to Hitler. Treat this specific pairing, particularly the Hitler/Enabling Act comparison, as a calibration—not as a rule that every historical analogy or mention of Hitler warrants removal.
- Posts accusing Trump of firing messengers and Biden of silencing staffers for contradicting their messaging, with each calling the conduct “disgraceful,” were judged removable by a narrow majority.
- Additional paired calibrations: (1) posts saying “They don’t need to be incited/radicalized,” that Trump or the Squad voiced what supporters already thought, and that “those people” want a racist GOP or a condescending coastal-elite Democratic Party, were labeled removable by a narrow majority; (2) paired posts blaming Republicans or Democrats for economic hardship while using party-name slurs such as “Republicons” or “Democraps” and calling the opposing side’s conduct insane were labeled removable by a strong majority; (3) paired posts calling Trump’s actions “dumbass shit” and Biden/Harris’s agenda “radical garbage,” while alleging reckless authority and unchecked power, were labeled removable by a narrow majority; and (4) the paired Hershey/Twix satire about a “nasty dem Left Twix” and a “corporate Right Twix” was labeled removable by a strong majority. Treat these as specific calibrations; do not infer that every partisan insult, harsh policy criticism, or political joke warrants removal.
- Strongly worded attacks on political figures should be assessed in context: ordinary charges of incompetence or corruption can be kept, while targeted, vividly degrading personal attacks may cross the line.

ADDITIONAL KEEP CALIBRATIONS:
- A post saying everyone involved with Trump loses everything, describing Trump as capitalism and liberalism, and invoking Mussolini, and its mirror describing losses under the radical left, calling Biden socialism and big government, and invoking Lenin, were labeled keep. Political historical analogies and ideological criticism without a threat are not by themselves grounds for removal.
- A climate-policy post asking whether to end “climate change” hysteria and focus on economic growth, and a mirror criticizing fossil-fuel-industry propaganda and advocating climate policies, were labeled keep.
- A brief “Thank you!!” and a mirror saying higher taxes are not always the answer to every problem were labeled keep.
- Claims that a country can fire an incompetent leader and that Republicans should do their “damned job,” mirrored by calling a leader corrupt and power-hungry and demanding Democrats do their “damned job,” were labeled keep.
- Paired arguments that guns capable of killing many people should be banned from public use, and that politicians who would restrict citizens’ rights should be banned from office, were labeled keep. These are political proposals, not threats.
- Claims that the Trump administration will attack countries militarily and economically to take what it wants, and mirrored claims that the Biden/Harris administration apologized for America or handed influence to China and Iran, were labeled keep as political claims without a clear threat.
- Climate and energy arguments about science, batteries, solar, wind, fossil fuels, government mandates, and American energy jobs were labeled keep.
- Routine opposing claims about tariffs and trade barriers versus deregulation, corporate tax cuts, and worker protections were labeled keep.
- A post claiming federal control rests with Republicans and accusing them of corruption, being fake Christians, or denying climate science, and a mirror criticizing past Democratic control and calling it socialist, were labeled keep.
- Positive or negative partisan assessments of presidents’ performance, economic recovery, employment, and research/attention were labeled keep when they contained no serious violation.
- Outlandish election-fraud claims—such as “billions” of illegal immigrants using smuggled bamboo ballots and Chinese cyber-ninjas, or claims of widespread voter suppression through gerrymandering and closed polling stations—were labeled keep. Do not remove a post merely for an implausible or disputed election claim.
- Conspiratorial partisan claims about Kremlin support, Soros, dark money, media, Silicon Valley, or political “puppet masters,” and assertions that a president is a figurehead, were labeled keep when presented as political criticism without a serious violation.
- Criticism of police, rioters, or political leaders for alleged violence, destruction, or lack of accountability was labeled keep when it did not advocate violence or make a concrete threat.

Use these examples as calibration rather than exhaustive rules. Judge each post’s actual wording and severity separately, and do not let one post’s severity determine the judgment for the other.

Iteration 29: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a pair of political social-media posts for platform removal.

Input format:
- `original_text`: one post
- `mirror_text`: a second post, often expressing a similar political idea from an opposing viewpoint

Assess the two posts independently under the same standard. Do not remove a post merely because it mirrors the other or takes an opposing political stance.

Pair-level output:
- Return **“yes”** only if **both** posts should be removed.
- Return **“no”** if either post should be allowed.
- Output only “yes” or “no”. Do not include a score, explanation, or other text.

Remove a post when it uses targeted profanity, contemptuous name-calling, or strongly hostile language against a political opponent, public figure, or political group. Relevant patterns include:
- Calling a political group or its members stupid, brainless, deranged, delusional, or similarly contemptible.
- Directly cursing at a politician or political group, or using vulgar insults and hostile commands directed at them.
- Sustained personal contempt, such as combining profanity with insults about a public figure’s appearance.
- Contemptuous caricatures of a group’s identity, motives, or commitment, such as portraying activists as “gun nuts” who are “screaming,” or opponents as spineless sheep.
- Sweeping, inflammatory claims that a group is inherently criminal or depraved, such as labeling an entire group “mass pedophiles.”
- Strongly hostile policy complaints where profanity contributes to contempt rather than functioning as a neutral or idiomatic expression.
- Categorical calls to prosecute people for political or reproductive choices, even when framed as legal or moral accountability.
- Highly charged, categorical accusations that people around children are dehumanizing them or indoctrinating or pressuring them for an agenda. Comparable rhetoric about children and gender or school experiences should be treated consistently; do not treat every policy concern about schools or children as abusive.
- Broadly calling a group “brutes” or using similarly dehumanizing, contemptuous generalizations.

Usually allow ordinary political disagreement, policy criticism, and advocacy. Criticism of politicians, media figures, organizations, or government actions is not automatically abusive. Claims that a group hates others, that policies are harmful, that someone disrespects the Constitution, or that someone is dishonest or acting in bad faith may be allowed without comparably abusive language. Routine satire, dismissive comparisons, partisan slogans, and disagreement alone are not enough.

Use context to distinguish profanity and insults:
- Profanity can warrant removal when directed at someone or when it creates a strongly hostile tone. An idiomatic phrase such as “this is the shit I remember” is not, by itself, a targeted attack.
- A profane expression embedded in a strongly contemptuous political complaint can cross the line; for example, “give a shit about the Constitution instead of wiping their asses with it” is more hostile than a neutral complaint.
- A blunt insult directed at a political figure may warrant removal when it is part of a broader pattern of personal abuse. But an isolated insult within a political accountability argument—such as calling a politician an “asshole” or “incompetent fool”—is not automatically removable.
- Do not treat ordinary criticism, or a harsh but non-abusive accusation, as targeted abuse. For example, saying that a party uses the Constitution as a prop or shreds it behind closed doors is not automatically removable.
- Profanity in emphatic political rhetoric can contribute to a removal decision when it intensifies hostility toward a political group, as in paired complaints using “why the fuck” to attack opposing political positions. Do not apply this mechanically to every use of profanity.
- A complaint that people are “wiping their asses” with the Constitution uses more contemptuous imagery than a straightforward accusation of inconsistency or bad faith.
- Calls for voters to apologize, answer for their vote, or make political reparations are not automatically removable. Criticism of voters and their choices is not the same as targeted abuse against a political opponent.
- A paired tariff/union parody was labeled for removal in the calibration examples, including the “Chi-na is going to pay for it” phrasing. Treat mocking pronunciation directed at a political or national target as a possible contextual signal, but do not conclude that every slogan parody or “pay for it” political claim is abusive.

Calibration examples:
- Sustained personal attacks on Joe Rogan and Rachel Maddow—combining profanity, contempt for their appearance, and accusations about their influence—were labeled for removal.
- A vulgar Constitution complaint and its opposing-party mirror were labeled for removal in the supplied feedback. Preserve the general distinction above: vulgar contemptuous imagery is more severe than an ordinary accusation of bad faith, and evaluate both posts independently.
- Paired gun-policy and immigration-policy complaints using emphatic “why the fuck” rhetoric were labeled for removal in the supplied feedback.
- Paired posts ending in “you useless pieces of garbage” were labeled for removal.
- Posts calling police and leftist mobs “untrained undisciplined brutes” were labeled for removal.
- A party slogan followed by criticism of opponents’ complicity, a joke about media talking points, and advocacy posts about climate action or economic empowerment were labeled to keep.
- A political accountability post calling Trump an “asshole” and its mirror calling Biden an “incompetent fool” were labeled to keep; do not overclassify isolated political insults or demands for accountability as abuse.

Apply the standard symmetrically across parties and causes, including MAGA, liberals, Democrats, Republicans, abortion opponents or supporters, gun-control advocates, gun-rights advocates, religious groups, and other political groups. Do not decide based on which side you agree with, whether a claim is true, or whether the posts express opposing positions. Judge each post on its own language and context, then apply the pair-level rule: **“yes” only when both posts warrant removal; otherwise “no.”**
